The libraries provide public access to a wide range of material, including online exhibits, digitized
collections, archival finding aids, our catalog, online articles, and a growing range of materials in many
media.
When possible, we provide rights information in catalog records, finding aids, and other metadata that
accompanies collections or items. However, it is always the user's obligation to evaluate copyright and
rights issues in light of their own use.

The sparkling new Wade Fetzer Academic Learning Center opened last spring to aid Badger student-athletes in the 
pursuit of their scholastic goals. 
The fully-equipped facility includes study rooms, a computer facility, auditorium, 
conference rooms and offices for the academic affairs staff.

Arlie Mucks (1914) 
First-team all-America honors by 
International News Service / Frank 
Menke 
One of Wisconsin’s truly great ath- 
letes, Arlie Mucks was probably best 
known for his success as a discus 
thrower. The 1914 football all- 
American (as selected by INS 
/ Frank 
Menke) placed second in the discus in 
the 1912 Olympics as a high school 
athlete. He was undefeated in college 
in the discus and shot put and set a 
then-world record discus mark of 155- 
2 in 1916. He is the only person to 
win national track titles in two events 
three consecutive years. Selected in 
1969 to Wisconsin’s all-time football 
team, Mucks officiated Big Ten foot- 
ball games following his playing 
career. He is a member of the State of 
Wisconsin and University of 
Wisconsin Athletic Halls of Fame.

Howard Weiss (1938) 
Zio

a 
First-team all-America honors by International News Service 
a 
After converting to fullback from halfback three games into his sophomore year, 
‘ 
Howard Weiss went on to earn Big Ten MVP and all-America honors in 1938. He was a 
first-team choice by INS and was a second-team choice by four organizations. He is per- 
haps best remembered for his 41-yard touchdown run through the entire Northwestern 
defense in a 1938 upset win at Evanston. Elected class president, Weiss was sixth in the 
1938 Heisman Trophy balloting and later played two years with the Detroit Lions.

Ray Snell (1979) 
First-team all-America honors by The Sporting News 
Baltimore native Ray Snell was a first-team all-America 
pick by The Sporting News as an offensive guard in 1979. The 
295-pound lineman, known for his speed and agility, blocked 
for Ira Matthews and Dave Mohapp at Wisconsin. Snell was 
the 22nd pick overall (by Tampa Bay) in the first round of the 
1980 NFL draft.

After playing just one year of high school football, Troy 
Former Badger center Cory Raymer with comedian Bob Hope. 
Vincent blossomed into one of the country’s best cornerbacks 
and punt returners. A 1991 all-America selection, Vincent was 
honored as the Big Ten’s co-Defensive Player of the Year after 
setting Badger season and career records for passes broken up 
(13 and 31). The UW’s record holder for career punt return 
yards returned punts for TDs his final three seasons. He was 
taken by Miami as the seventh overall choice in the 1992 draft.

Tarek Saleh (1996) 
RETIRED NUMBERS 
First-team all-America honors by Football News 
A two-time consensus first-team all-Big Ten selection, 
Tarek Saleh earned all-America honors from Football News 
after his senior season. Saleh paced the Big Ten in quarter- 
back sacks in both 1995 and 1996. He also topped the con- 
3 4 
4 () 
ference in tackles for loss as a junior and finished third as a 
senior, Saleh, a 240-pound defensive end, concluded his 
Wisconsin career as the school’s career record holder in 
tackles for loss (58), yardage (283), quarterback sacks (33) 
and yardage (227). 
He also established UW season marks 
for sacks (14) and yardage (97) during the ‘96 campaign. 
Saleh, who participated in the East-West Shrine Game, was 
named Copper Bowl defensive Most Valuable Player in his

Pictured above (left to right) are Al Toon (the school’s all-time receiving leader), Reggie Torian (two-year football letterman) and Tony 
Simmons (No. 5 all-time receiver). 
All three made their mark at the UW as track stars, too. Torian won an NCAA championship and 
then finished second at the U.S. Track and Field Championships in the 110-meter high hurdles, while Toon and Simmons won Big Ten 
titles in the triple jump and sprints at the UW. Torian is currently running world-level track on the European circuit.

Ron Dayne surpassed the UW season record registering 126 points in 1996. That mark 
crushed the previous Badger freshman mark of 84 points set by Todd Gregoire. Dayne 
scored 11 rushing TDs in the final three games.

The University of Wisconsin became the first team in Big Ten history to produce a different 1,000-yard rusher 
four years in a row when freshman Ron Dayne gained 2,109 yards in 1996. Brent Moss (a junior), Terrell Fletcher 
(a senior) and Carl McCullough (a sophomore) joined the 1,000-yard club in 1993, 1994 and 1995, respectively.

beltline to Park Street (Hwy. 151).
